<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/150/#laws.3.2.0">2014 c 150 art 3 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/308/#laws.11.3.0">2014 c 308 art 11 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/1/#laws.13.5.0">1Sp2017 c 1 art 13 s 5</a>; art 16 s 26; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/0/50/#laws.1.89.0">2019 c 50 art 1 s 89</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/64/#laws.7.4.0">2023 c 64 art 7 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendment to subdivision 5 by Laws 2023, chapter 64, article 7, section 4, is effective for property taxes payable in 2025 and thereafter. Laws 2023, chapter 64, article 7, section 4, the effective date.</p>
      <div class="section" id="stat.289A.19">
        <h1 class="shn">289A.19 EXTENSIONS FOR FILING RETURNS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.19.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Fiduciary income, entertainment tax, and information returns.</span></h2>
          <p>When, in the commissioner's judgment, good cause exists, the commissioner may extend the time for filing entertainment tax returns for not more than six months. The commissioner shall grant an automatic extension of six months to file a partnership, "S" corporation, or fiduciary income tax return if all of the taxes imposed on the entity for the year by chapter 290 and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.08#stat.289A.08.7">289A.08, subdivision 7</a>, have been paid by the date prescribed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.18#stat.289A.18.1">289A.18, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.19.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Corporate franchise and mining company taxes.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Corporations or mining companies shall receive an extension of seven months or the amount of time granted by the Internal Revenue Service, whichever is longer, for filing the return of a corporation subject to tax under chapter 290 or for filing the return of a mining company subject to tax under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/298.01">298.01</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/298.015">298.015</a>. Interest on any balance of tax not paid when the regularly required return is due must be paid at the rate specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.40">270C.40</a>, from the date such payment should have been made if no extension was granted, until the date of payment of such tax.</p>
          <p class="in">If a corporation or mining company does not:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) pay at least 90 percent of the amount of tax shown on the return on or before the regular due date of the return, the penalty prescribed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.60#stat.289A.60.1">289A.60, subdivision 1</a>, shall be imposed on the unpaid balance of tax; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) pay the balance due shown on the regularly required return on or before the extended due date of the return, the penalty prescribed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.60#stat.289A.60.1">289A.60, subdivision 1</a>, shall be imposed on the unpaid balance of tax from the original due date of the return.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.19.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Withholding returns.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall grant an automatic extension of 60 days to file a withholding tax return with the commissioner provided all the withholding taxes have been paid by the date prescribed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.20#stat.289A.20.2">289A.20, subdivision 2</a>. In any case where good cause exists, the commissioner may grant an extension of time of not more than 60 days for filing a withholding return.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.19.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Estate tax returns.</span></h2>
          <p>The time for filing an estate tax return shall be extended for either six months or the amount of time granted under section 6081 of the Internal Revenue Code to file the federal estate tax return, whichever is longer.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.19.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Sales and use tax returns.</span></h2>
          <p>Where good cause exists, the commissioner may extend the time for filing sales and use tax returns for not more than 60 days.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.19.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/291/#laws.6.47.0">1991 c 291 art 6 s 47</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.19.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Federal extensions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">When an extension of time to file a partnership, fiduciary income tax, or S corporation tax return is granted by the Internal Revenue Service, the commissioner shall grant an automatic extension to file the comparable Minnesota return for that period. An extension granted under this subdivision does not affect the due date for making payments of tax.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.289A.19.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/480/#laws.1.9.0">1990 c 480 art 1 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/291/#laws.6.9.0">1991 c 291 art 6 s 9</a>,46; art 11 s 5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/511/#laws.6.19.0">1992 c 511 art 6 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/375/#laws.8.14.0">1993 c 375 art 8 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/587/#laws.1.24.0">1994 c 587 art 1 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/31/#laws.1.7.0">1997 c 31 art 1 s 7</a>-10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/389/#laws.6.1.0">1998 c 389 art 6 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/377/#laws.9.6.0">2002 c 377 art 9 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/127/#laws.3.2.0">2003 c 127 art 3 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.2.17.0">2005 c 151 art 2 s 17</a>; art 6 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/366/#laws.4.1.0">2008 c 366 art 4 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/88/#laws.7.4.0">2009 c 88 art 7 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/1/#laws.1.3.0">1Sp2017 c 1 art 1 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.289A.20">
        <h1 class="shn">289A.20 DUE DATES FOR MAKING PAYMENTS OF TAX.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.20.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Individual income, fiduciary income, mining company, corporate franchise, and entertainment taxes.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Individual income, fiduciary, mining company, and corporate franchise taxes must be paid to the commissioner on or before the date the return must be filed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.18#stat.289A.18.1">289A.18, subdivision 1</a>, or the extended due date as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.19">289A.19</a>, unless an earlier date for payment is provided.</p>
          <p>Notwithstanding any other law, a taxpayer whose unpaid liability for income or corporate franchise taxes, as reflected upon the return, is $1 or less need not pay the tax.</p>
          <p>(b) Entertainment taxes must be paid on or before the date the return must be filed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.18#stat.289A.18.1">289A.18, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) If a fiduciary administers 100 or more trusts, fiduciary income taxes for all trusts administered by the fiduciary must be paid by electronic means.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.20.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Withholding from wages, entertainer withholding, withholding from payments to out-of-state contractors, and withholding by partnerships, small business corporations, trusts.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.18#stat.289A.18.2a">289A.18, subdivision 2a</a>, a tax required to be deducted and withheld during the quarterly period must be paid on or before the last day of the month following the close of the quarterly period, unless an earlier time for payment is provided. A tax required to be deducted and withheld from compensation of an entertainer and from a payment to an out-of-state contractor must be paid on or before the date the return for such tax must be filed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.18#stat.289A.18.2">289A.18, subdivision 2</a>. Taxes required to be deducted and withheld by partnerships, S corporations, and trusts must be paid on a quarterly basis as estimated taxes under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.25">289A.25</a> for partnerships and trusts and under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.26">289A.26</a> for S corporations.</p>
          <p>(b) An employer who, during the previous quarter, withheld more than $1,500 of tax under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.92#stat.290.92.2a">290.92, subdivision 2a</a> or 3, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.923#stat.290.923.2">290.923, subdivision 2</a>, must deposit tax withheld under those sections with the commissioner within the time allowed to deposit the employer's federal withheld employment taxes under Code of Federal Regulations, title 26, section 31.6302-1, as amended through December 31, 2001, without regard to the safe harbor or de minimis rules in paragraph (f) or the one-day rule in paragraph (c)(3). Taxpayers must submit a copy of their federal notice of deposit status to the commissioner upon request by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner may prescribe by rule other return periods or deposit requirements. In prescribing the reporting period, the commissioner may classify payors according to the amount of their tax liability and may adopt an appropriate reporting period for the class that the commissioner judges to be consistent with efficient tax collection. In no event will the duration of the reporting period be more than one year.</p>
          <p>(d) If less than the correct amount of tax is paid to the commissioner, proper adjustments with respect to both the tax and the amount to be deducted must be made, without interest, in the manner and at the times the commissioner prescribes. If the underpayment cannot be adjusted, the amount of the underpayment will be assessed and collected in the manner and at the times the commissioner prescribes.</p>
          <p>(e) If the aggregate amount of the tax withheld is $10,000 or more in a fiscal year ending June 30, the employer must remit each required deposit for wages paid in all subsequent calendar years by electronic means.</p>
          <p>(f) A third-party bulk filer as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.92#stat.290.92.30">290.92, subdivision 30</a>, paragraph (a), clause (2), who remits withholding deposits must remit all deposits by electronic means as provided in paragraph (e), regardless of the aggregate amount of tax withheld during a fiscal year for all of the employers.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.20.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Estate tax.</span></h2>
          <p>Taxes imposed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/291.03#stat.291.03.1">291.03, subdivision 1</a>, take effect at and upon the death of the person whose estate is subject to taxation and are due and payable on or before the expiration of nine months from that death.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.20.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Recapture tax.</span></h2>
          <p>The additional estate tax imposed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/291.03">291.03</a>, subdivision 11, paragraph (b), is due and payable on or before the expiration of the date provided by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/291.03#stat.291.03.11">291.03, subdivision 11</a>, paragraph (c).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.20.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Sales and use tax.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The taxes imposed by chapter 297A are due and payable to the commissioner monthly on or before the 20th day of the month following the month in which the taxable event occurred, or following another reporting period as the commissioner prescribes or as allowed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.18#stat.289A.18.4">289A.18, subdivision 4</a>, paragraph (f) or (g), except that use taxes due on an annual use tax return as provided under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.11#stat.289A.11.1">289A.11, subdivision 1</a>, are payable by April 15 following the close of the calendar year.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A vendor having a liability of $250,000 or more during a fiscal year ending June 30, except a vendor of construction materials as defined in paragraph (e), must remit the June liability for the next year in the following manner:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) Two business days before June 30 of calendar year 2020 and 2021, the vendor must remit 87.5 percent of the estimated June liability to the commissioner. Two business days before June 30 of calendar year 2022 and thereafter, the vendor must remit 84.5 percent, or a reduced percentage as certified by the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.152">16A.152, subdivision </a>2, paragraph (a), clause (6), of the estimated June liability to the commissioner.</p>
          <p class="in">(2) On or before August 20 of the year, the vendor must pay any additional amount of tax not remitted in June.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) A vendor having a liability of:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) $10,000 or more, but less than $250,000, during a fiscal year must remit by electronic means all liabilities on returns due for periods beginning in all subsequent calendar years on or before the 20th day of the month following the month in which the taxable event occurred, or on or before the 20th day of the month following the month in which the sale is reported under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.18#stat.289A.18.4">289A.18, subdivision 4</a>; or</p>
          <p>(2) $250,000 or more during a fiscal year must remit by electronic means all liabilities in the manner provided in paragraph (a) on returns due for periods beginning in the subsequent calendar year, except that a vendor subject to the remittance requirements of paragraph (b) must remit the percentage of the estimated June liability, as provided in paragraph (b), clause (1), which is due two business days before June 30. The remaining amount of the June liability is due on August 20.</p>
          <p>(d) Notwithstanding paragraph (b) or (c), a person prohibited by the person's religious beliefs from paying electronically shall be allowed to remit the payment by mail. The filer must notify the commissioner of revenue of the intent to pay by mail before doing so on a form prescribed by the commissioner. No extra fee may be charged to a person making payment by mail under this paragraph. The payment must be postmarked at least two business days before the due date for making the payment in order to be considered paid on a timely basis.</p>
          <p>(e) For the purposes of paragraph (b), "vendor of construction materials" means a retailer that sells any of the following construction materials, if 50 percent or more of the retailer's sales revenue for the fiscal year ending June 30 is from the sale of those materials:</p>
          <p>(1) lumber, veneer, plywood, wood siding, wood roofing;</p>
          <p>(2) millwork, including wood trim, wood doors, wood windows, wood flooring; or</p>
          <p>(3) concrete, cement, and masonry.</p>
          <p>(f) Paragraph (b) expires after the percentage of estimated payment is reduced to zero in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.152#stat.16A.152.2">16A.152, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (a), clause (6).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.20.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Payment of franchise tax on LIFO recapture.</span></h2>
          <p>If a corporation is subject to LIFO recapture under section 1363(d) of the Internal Revenue Code, any increase in the tax imposed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.06#stat.290.06.1">290.06, subdivision 1</a>, by reason of the inclusion of the LIFO recapture amount in its income is payable in four equal installments.</p>
          <p>The first installment must be paid on or before the due date, determined without regard to extensions, for filing the return for the first taxable year for which the corporation was subject to the LIFO recapture. The three succeeding installments must be paid on or before the due date, determined without regard to extensions, for filing the corporation's return for the three succeeding taxable years.</p>
          <p>For purposes of computing interest on underpayments, the last three installments must not be considered underpayments until after the payment due date specified in this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.289A.20.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>

      <h1 class="fgh">PAYMENT OF ESTIMATED TAX</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.289A.25">
        <h1 class="shn">289A.25 PAYMENT OF ESTIMATED TAX BY INDIVIDUALS, TRUSTS, S CORPORATIONS, OR PARTNERSHIPS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.25.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Requirements to pay.</span></h2>
          <p>An individual, trust, S corporation, or partnership must, when prescribed in subdivision 3, paragraph (b), make payments of estimated tax. For individuals, the term "estimated tax" means the amount the taxpayer estimates is the sum of the taxes imposed by chapter 290 for the taxable year. For trusts, S corporations, and partnerships, the term estimated tax means the amount the taxpayer estimates is the sum of the taxes for the taxable year imposed by chapter 290 and the composite income tax imposed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.08#stat.289A.08.7">289A.08, subdivision 7</a>. If the individual is an infant or incompetent person, the payments must be made by the individual's guardian. If joint payments on estimated tax are made but a joint return is not made for the taxable year, the estimated tax for that year may be treated as the estimated tax of either spouse or may be divided between them.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.25.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Additions to tax for underpayment.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) In the case of any underpayment of estimated tax by a taxpayer, except as provided in subdivision 6 or 7, there must be added to and become a part of the taxes imposed by chapter 290, for the taxable year an amount determined at the rate specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.40">270C.40</a> upon the amount of the underpayment for the period of the underpayment.</p>
          <p>(b) For purposes of paragraph (a), the amount of underpayment shall be the excess of</p>
          <p>(1) the amount of the installment required to be paid, over</p>
          <p>(2) the amount, if any, of the installment paid on or before the last day prescribed for the payment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.25.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Period of underpayment.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The period of the underpayment shall run from the date the installment was required to be paid to the earlier of the following dates:</p>
          <p>(1) The 15th day of the fourth month following the close of the taxable year.</p>
          <p>(2) With respect to any part of the underpayment, the date on which that part is paid. For purposes of this clause, a payment of estimated tax on any installment date is considered a payment of any unpaid required installments in the order in which the installments are required to be paid.</p>
          <p>(b) For purposes of this subdivision, there shall be four required installments for a taxable year. The times for payment of installments shall be:</p>
          <table>
            <col style="width:9.016393442622952%">
            <col style="width:8.196721311475411%">
            <col style="width:16.3934426229508%">
            <col style="width:16.3934426229508%">
            <col style="width:50%">
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td colspan="2">For the following required installments:</td>
              <td></td>
              <td>The due date is:</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td></td>
              <td>1st</td>
              <td></td>
              <td>April 15</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td></td>
              <td>2nd</td>
              <td></td>
              <td>June 15</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td></td>
              <td>3rd</td>
              <td></td>
              <td>September 15</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td></td>
              <td class="valign_top">4th</td>
              <td></td>
              <td>January 15 of the following taxable year</td>
            </tr>
          </table>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.25.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">No addition to tax where tax is small.</span></h2>
          <p>No addition to tax is imposed under subdivision 2 for a taxable year if the tax shown on the return for the taxable year (or, if no return is filed, the tax) reduced by the credits allowable is less than $500.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.25.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Amount of required installment.</span></h2>
          <p>The amount of any installment required to be paid shall be 25 percent of the required annual payment except as provided in clause (3). The term "required annual payment" means the lesser of</p>
          <p>(1) 90 percent of the tax shown on the return for the taxable year or 90 percent of the tax for the year if no return is filed; or</p>
          <p>(2) the total tax liability shown on the return of the taxpayer for the preceding taxable year, if a return showing a liability for the taxes was filed by the taxpayer for the preceding taxable year of 12 months. If the adjusted gross income shown on the return of the taxpayer for the preceding taxable year exceeds $150,000, this clause shall be applied by substituting "110 percent of the total tax liability" for "the total tax liability"</p>
          <p>(i) for an individual who is not a Minnesota resident for the entire year, the term "adjusted gross income" means the Minnesota share of that income apportioned to Minnesota under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.06#stat.290.06.2c">290.06, subdivision 2c</a>, paragraph (e), or</p>
          <p>(ii) for a trust the term "adjusted gross income" means the income assigned to Minnesota under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.17">290.17</a>; or</p>
          <p>(3) an amount equal to the applicable percentage of the tax for the taxable year computed by placing on an annualized basis the taxable income and alternative minimum taxable income for the months in the taxable year ending before the month in which the installment is required to be paid. The applicable percentage of the tax is 22.5 percent in the case of the first installment, 45 percent for the second installment, 67.5 percent for the third installment, and 90 percent for the fourth installment. For purposes of this clause, the taxable income and alternative minimum taxable income shall be placed on an annualized basis by</p>
          <p>(i) multiplying by 12 (or in the case of a taxable year of less than 12 months, the number of months in the taxable year) the taxable income and alternative minimum taxable income computed for the months in the taxable year ending before the month in which the installment is required to be paid; and</p>
          <p>(ii) dividing the resulting amount by the number of months in the taxable year ending before the month in which the installment date falls.</p>
          <p class="in">A reduction in an installment under clause (3) must be recaptured by increasing the amount of the next required installment by the amount of the reduction.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.25.5a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5a.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/587/#laws.1.25.0">1994 c 587 art 1 s 25</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.25.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Exception to addition to tax.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For individuals, no addition to the tax is imposed under this section for any taxable year if:</p>
          <p>(1) the taxpayer did not have liability for tax for the preceding taxable year,</p>
          <p>(2) the preceding taxable year was a taxable year of 12 months, and</p>
          <p>(3) the individual was a resident of Minnesota throughout the preceding taxable year.</p>
          <p>(b) For trusts, S corporations, and partnerships, if in any previous taxable year the entity was subject to taxation under chapter 290 or composite income tax is elected under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.08#stat.289A.08.7">289A.08, subdivision 7</a>, then an addition to the tax is imposed under this section. In all other taxable years, no addition to tax is imposed under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.25.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Waiver of addition to tax.</span></h2>
          <p>No addition to the tax is imposed under this section with respect to an underpayment to the extent the commissioner determines that the provisions of section 6654(e)(3) of the Internal Revenue Code apply.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.25.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Application of section; tax withheld on wages.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section, the estimated tax must be computed without reduction for the amount that the taxpayer estimates as the taxpayer's credit under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.92#stat.290.92.12">290.92, subdivision 12</a> (relating to tax withheld at source on wages), and any other refundable credits allowed against income tax liability, and the amount of those credits for the taxable year is considered a payment of estimated tax, and an equal part of those amounts is considered paid on the installment date, determined under subdivision 3, paragraph (b), for that taxable year, unless the taxpayer establishes the dates on which the amounts were actually withheld, in which case the amounts so withheld are considered payments of estimated tax on the dates on which the amounts were actually withheld.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.25.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Special rule for return filed on or before January 31.</span></h2>
          <p>If, on or before January 31 of the following taxable year, the taxpayer files a return for the taxable year and pays in full the amount computed on the return as payable, then no addition to tax is imposed under subdivision 2 with respect to any underpayment of the fourth required installment for the taxable year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.25.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Special rule for farmers and fishermen.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section, if an individual is a farmer or fisherman as defined in section 6654(i)(2) of the Internal Revenue Code for a taxable year, only one installment is required for the taxable year, the due date of which is January 15 of the following taxable year, the amount of which is equal to the required annual payment determined under subdivision 5 by substituting "66-2/3 percent" for "90 percent," and subdivision 9 shall be applied by substituting "March 1" for "January 31," and by treating the required installment described as the fourth required installment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.25.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Fiscal year taxpayer.</span></h2>
          <p>The application of this section to taxable years beginning other than January 1 must be made by substituting, for the months named in this section, the months that correspond. This section must be applied to taxable years of less than 12 months, under rules issued by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.25.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Estates.</span></h2>
          <p>The provisions of this section do not apply to an estate.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.25.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Overpayment of estimated tax installment.</span></h2>
          <p>If an installment payment of estimated tax exceeds the correct amount of the installment payment, the overpayment must be credited against the unpaid installments, if any.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.25.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Short taxable year.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A trust, S corporation, or partnership with a short taxable year of less than 12 months, but at least four months, must pay estimated tax in equal installments on or before the 15th day of the third, sixth, ninth, and final month of the short taxable year, to the extent applicable based on the number of months in the short taxable year.</p>
          <p>(b) A trust, S corporation, or partnership is not required to make estimated tax payments for a short taxable year unless its tax liability before the first day of the last month of the taxable year can reasonably be expected to exceed $500.</p>
          <p>(c) No payment is required by a trust, S corporation, or partnership for a short taxable year of less than four months.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.289A.25.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/480/#laws.1.11.0">1990 c 480 art 1 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/291/#laws.6.46.0">1991 c 291 art 6 s 46</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/511/#laws.6.3.0">1992 c 511 art 6 s 3</a>,19; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/375/#laws.2.22.0">1993 c 375 art 2 s 22</a>-28; art 8 s 14; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/416/#laws.2.2.0">1994 c 416 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/587/#laws.1.4.0">1994 c 587 art 1 s 4</a>,24; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.2.17.0">2005 c 151 art 2 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/112/#laws.1.5.0">2011 c 112 art 1 s 5</a>-7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/308/#laws.9.59.0">2014 c 308 art 9 s 59</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/6/#laws.24.7.0">1Sp2019 c 6 art 24 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.289A.26">
        <h1 class="shn">289A.26 PAYMENT OF ESTIMATED TAX BY CORPORATIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.26.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Minimum liability.</span></h2>
          <p>A corporation subject to taxation under chapter 290 (excluding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.92">290.92</a> and an S corporation under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.9725">290.9725</a>) or an entity subject to taxation under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.05#stat.290.05.3">290.05, subdivision 3</a>, must make payment of estimated tax for the taxable year if its tax liability so computed can reasonably be expected to exceed $500, or in accordance with rules prescribed by the commissioner for an affiliated group of corporations filing one return under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.08#stat.289A.08.3">289A.08, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.26.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Amount and time for payment of installments.</span></h2>
          <p>The estimated tax payment required under subdivision 1 must be paid in four equal installments on or before the 15th day of the third, sixth, ninth, and 12th month of the taxable year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.26.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Electronic payments.</span></h2>
          <p>If the aggregate amount of estimated tax payments made is $10,000 or more in a fiscal year ending June 30, all estimated tax payments in all subsequent calendar years must be paid by electronic means.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.26.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Short taxable year.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A corporation or an entity with a short taxable year of less than 12 months, but at least four months, must pay estimated tax in equal installments on or before the 15th day of the third, sixth, ninth, and final month of the short taxable year, to the extent applicable based on the number of months in the short taxable year.</p>
          <p>(b) A corporation or an entity is not required to make estimated tax payments for a short taxable year unless its tax liability before the first day of the last month of the taxable year can reasonably be expected to exceed $500.</p>
          <p>(c) No payment is required for a short taxable year of less than four months.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.26.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Underpayment of estimated tax.</span></h2>
          <p>If there is an underpayment of estimated tax by a corporation or an entity, there shall be added to the tax for the taxable year an amount determined at the rate in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.40">270C.40</a> on the amount of the underpayment, determined under subdivision 5, for the period of the underpayment determined under subdivision 6. This subdivision does not apply in the first taxable year that a corporation is subject to the tax imposed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.02">290.02</a> or an entity is subject to the tax imposed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.05#stat.290.05.3">290.05, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.26.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Amount of underpayment.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of subdivision 4, the amount of the underpayment is the excess of</p>
          <p>(1) the required installment, over</p>
          <p>(2) the amount, if any, of the installment paid on or before the last date prescribed for payment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.26.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Period of underpayment.</span></h2>
          <p>The period of the underpayment runs from the date the installment was required to be paid to the earlier of the following dates:</p>
          <p>(1) the 15th day of the third month following the close of the taxable year for corporations, and the 15th day of the fifth month following the close of the taxable year for entities subject to tax under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.05#stat.290.05.3">290.05, subdivision 3</a>; or</p>
          <p>(2) with respect to any part of the underpayment, the date on which that part is paid. For purposes of this clause, a payment of estimated tax shall be credited against unpaid required installments in the order in which those installments are required to be paid.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.26.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Required installments.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except as otherwise provided in this subdivision, the amount of a required installment is 25 percent of the required annual payment.</p>
          <p>(b) Except as otherwise provided in this subdivision, the term "required annual payment" means the lesser of:</p>
          <p>(1) 100 percent of the tax shown on the return for the taxable year, or, if no return is filed, 100 percent of the tax for that year; or</p>
          <p>(2) 100 percent of the tax shown on the return of the corporation or entity for the preceding taxable year provided the return was for a full 12-month period, showed a liability, and was filed by the corporation or entity.</p>
          <p>(c) Except for determining the first required installment for any taxable year, paragraph (b), clause (2), does not apply in the case of a large corporation. The term "large corporation" means a corporation or any predecessor corporation that had taxable net income of $1,000,000 or more for any taxable year during the testing period. The term "testing period" means the three taxable years immediately preceding the taxable year involved. A reduction allowed to a large corporation for the first installment that is allowed by applying paragraph (b), clause (2), must be recaptured by increasing the next required installment by the amount of the reduction.</p>
          <p>(d) In the case of a required installment, if the corporation or entity establishes that the annualized income installment is less than the amount determined in paragraph (a), the amount of the required installment is the annualized income installment and the recapture of previous quarters' reductions allowed by this paragraph must be recovered by increasing later required installments to the extent the reductions have not previously been recovered.</p>
          <p>(e) The "annualized income installment" is the excess, if any, of:</p>
          <p>(1) an amount equal to the applicable percentage of the tax for the taxable year computed by placing on an annualized basis the taxable income:</p>
          <p>(i) for the first two months of the taxable year, in the case of the first required installment;</p>
          <p>(ii) for the first two months or for the first five months of the taxable year, in the case of the second required installment;</p>
          <p>(iii) for the first six months or for the first eight months of the taxable year, in the case of the third required installment; and</p>
          <p>(iv) for the first nine months or for the first 11 months of the taxable year, in the case of the fourth required installment, over</p>
          <p>(2) the aggregate amount of any prior required installments for the taxable year.</p>
          <p>(3) For the purpose of this paragraph, the annualized income shall be computed by placing on an annualized basis the taxable income for the year up to the end of the month preceding the due date for the quarterly payment multiplied by 12 and dividing the resulting amount by the number of months in the taxable year (2, 5, 6, 8, 9, or 11 as the case may be) referred to in clause (1).</p>
          <p>(4) The "applicable percentage" used in clause (1) is:</p>
          <table>
            <col style="width:8%">
            <col style="width:8%">
            <col style="width:16%">
            <col style="width:24%">
            <col style="width:12%">
            <col style="width:32%">
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td colspan="2">For the following required installments:</td>
              <td></td>
              <td colspan="2">The applicable<br>percentage is:</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td></td>
              <td>1st</td>
              <td></td>
              <td style="text-align: right;">25</td>
              <td></td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td></td>
              <td>2nd</td>
              <td></td>
              <td style="text-align: right;">50</td>
              <td></td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td></td>
              <td>3rd</td>
              <td></td>
              <td style="text-align: right;">75</td>
              <td></td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td></td>
              <td class="valign_top">4th</td>
              <td></td>
              <td style="text-align: right;">100</td>
              <td></td>
            </tr>
          </table>
          <p>(f)(1) If this paragraph applies, the amount determined for any installment must be determined in the following manner:</p>
          <p>(i) take the taxable income for the months during the taxable year preceding the filing month;</p>
          <p>(ii) divide that amount by the base period percentage for the months during the taxable year preceding the filing month;</p>
          <p>(iii) determine the tax on the amount determined under item (ii); and</p>
          <p>(iv) multiply the tax computed under item (iii) by the base period percentage for the filing month and the months during the taxable year preceding the filing month.</p>
          <p>(2) For purposes of this paragraph:</p>
          <p>(i) the "base period percentage" for a period of months is the average percent that the taxable income for the corresponding months in each of the three preceding taxable years bears to the taxable income for the three preceding taxable years;</p>
          <p>(ii) the term "filing month" means the month in which the installment is required to be paid;</p>
          <p>(iii) this paragraph only applies if the base period percentage for any six consecutive months of the taxable year equals or exceeds 70 percent; and</p>
          <p>(iv) the commissioner may provide by rule for the determination of the base period percentage in the case of reorganizations, new corporations or entities, and other similar circumstances.</p>
          <p>(3) In the case of a required installment determined under this paragraph, if the corporation or entity determines that the installment is less than the amount determined in paragraph (a), the amount of the required installment is the amount determined under this paragraph and the recapture of previous quarters' reductions allowed by this paragraph must be recovered by increasing later required installments to the extent the reductions have not previously been recovered.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.26.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Definition of tax.</span></h2>
          <p>The term "tax" as used in this section means the tax imposed by chapter 290.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.26.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Failure to file an estimate.</span></h2>
          <p>In the case of a corporation or an entity that fails to file an estimated tax for a taxable year when one is required, the period of the underpayment runs from the four installment dates in subdivision 2 or 3, whichever applies, to the earlier of the periods in subdivision 6, clauses (1) and (2).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.26.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Payment on account.</span></h2>
          <p>Payment of the estimated tax or any installment of it shall be considered payment on account of the taxes imposed by chapter 290, for the taxable year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.26.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Overpayment of estimated tax installment.</span></h2>
          <p>If the amount of an installment payment of estimated tax exceeds the amount determined to be the correct amount of the installment payment, the overpayment must be credited against the unpaid installments, if any.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.289A.26.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/480/#laws.1.12.0">1990 c 480 art 1 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/291/#laws.7.2.0">1991 c 291 art 7 s 2</a>,3; art 17 s 5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/511/#laws.6.4.0">1992 c 511 art 6 s 4</a>-9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/375/#laws.2.29.0">1993 c 375 art 2 s 29</a>-31; art 8 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/587/#laws.1.5.0">1994 c 587 art 1 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/264/#laws.13.10.0">1995 c 264 art 13 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/490/#laws.4.3.0">2000 c 490 art 4 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.17.12.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 17 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.2.17.0">2005 c 151 art 2 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/3/#laws.9.3.0">1Sp2005 c 3 art 9 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/112/#laws.1.8.0">2011 c 112 art 1 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/142/#laws.5.6.0">2013 c 142 art 5 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/143/#laws.15.7.0">2013 c 143 art 15 s 7</a>-10</p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">PAYMENT EXTENSIONS AND LIABILITY</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.289A.30">
        <h1 class="shn">289A.30 EXTENSIONS FOR PAYING TAX.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.30.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Fiduciary income, corporate franchise tax.</span></h2>
          <p>Where good cause exists, the commissioner may extend the time for payment of the amount determined as a fiduciary income tax or corporate franchise tax by the taxpayer, or an amount determined as a deficiency, for a period of not more than six months from the date prescribed for the payment of the tax.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.30.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Estate tax.</span></h2>
          <p>Where good cause exists, the commissioner may extend the time for payment of estate tax for a period of not more than six months. If an extension to pay the federal estate tax has been granted under section 6161 of the Internal Revenue Code, the time for payment of the estate tax without penalty is extended for that period. A taxpayer who owes at least $5,000 in taxes and who, under section 6161 or 6166 of the Internal Revenue Code has been granted an extension for payment of the tax shown on the return, may elect to pay the tax due to the commissioner in equal amounts at the same time as required for federal purposes. A taxpayer electing to pay the tax in installments shall defer a percentage of tax that does not exceed the percentage of federal tax deferred and must notify the commissioner in writing no later than nine months after the death of the person whose estate is subject to taxation. If the taxpayer fails to pay an installment on time, unless it is shown that the failure is due to reasonable cause, the election is revoked and the entire amount of unpaid tax plus accrued interest is due and payable 90 days after the date on which the installment was payable.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.289A.30.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/480/#laws.1.13.0">1990 c 480 art 1 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/291/#laws.6.11.0">1991 c 291 art 6 s 11</a>,46; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/511/#laws.6.19.0">1992 c 511 art 6 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/375/#laws.8.14.0">1993 c 375 art 8 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/587/#laws.1.24.0">1994 c 587 art 1 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/389/#laws.3.5.0">2010 c 389 art 3 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.289A.31">
        <h1 class="shn">289A.31 LIABILITY FOR PAYMENT OF TAX.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.31.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Individual income, fiduciary income, mining company, corporate franchise, and entertainment taxes.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Individual income, fiduciary income, mining company, and corporate franchise taxes, and interest and penalties, must be paid by the taxpayer upon whom the tax is imposed, except in the following cases:</p>
          <p>(1) the tax due from a decedent for that part of the taxable year in which the decedent died during which the decedent was alive and the taxes, interest, and penalty due for the prior years must be paid by the decedent's personal representative, if any. If there is no personal representative, the taxes, interest, and penalty must be paid by the transferees, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.58#stat.270C.58.3">270C.58, subdivision 3</a>, to the extent they receive property from the decedent;</p>
          <p>(2) the tax due from an infant or other incompetent person must be paid by the person's guardian or other person authorized or permitted by law to act for the person;</p>
          <p>(3) the tax due from the estate of a decedent must be paid by the estate's personal representative;</p>
          <p>(4) the tax due from a trust, including those within the definition of a corporation, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.01#stat.290.01.4">290.01, subdivision 4</a>, must be paid by a trustee; and</p>
          <p>(5) the tax due from a taxpayer whose business or property is in charge of a receiver, trustee in bankruptcy, assignee, or other conservator, must be paid by the person in charge of the business or property so far as the tax is due to the income from the business or property.</p>
          <p>(b) Entertainment taxes are the joint and several liability of the entertainer and the entertainment entity. The payor is liable to the state for the payment of the tax required to be deducted and withheld under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.9201#stat.290.9201.7">290.9201, subdivision 7</a>, and is not liable to the entertainer for the amount of the payment.</p>
          <p>(c) The taxes imposed under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.35">289A.35</a>, paragraph (b), <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.382">289A.382, subdivision </a>3, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.0922">290.0922</a> on partnerships are the joint and several liability of the partnership and the general partners.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.31.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Joint income tax returns.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If a joint income tax return is made by spouses, the liability for the tax is joint and several. A spouse who qualifies for relief from a liability attributable to an underpayment under section 6015 subsection (b) of the Internal Revenue Code, or determined by the commissioner of internal revenue for relief under section 6015 subsection (f) of the Internal Revenue Code, is relieved of the state income tax liability on the underpayment.</p>
          <p>(b) In the case of individuals who were married as determined in section 7703 of the Internal Revenue Code prior to the dissolution of their marriage or their legal separation, or prior to the death of one of the individuals, for tax liabilities reported on a joint or combined return, the liability of each person is limited to the proportion of the tax due on the return that equals that person's proportion of the total tax due if each spouse filed separate returns for the taxable year. This provision is effective only when the commissioner receives written notice of the marriage dissolution, legal separation, or death of a spouse from the surviving spouse. No refund may be claimed by an ex-spouse, legally separated or widowed spouse for any taxes paid more than 60 days before receipt by the commissioner of the written notice.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner is not required to calculate separate liability pursuant to paragraph (b) if the remaining unpaid liability for which recalculation is requested is $100 or less.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.31.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.117.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 117</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.31.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.117.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 117</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.31.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Withholding tax, withholding from payments to out-of-state contractors, and withholding by partnerships and small business corporations.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except as provided in paragraph (b), an employer or person withholding tax under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.92">290.92</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.923#stat.290.923.2">290.923, subdivision 2</a>, who fails to pay to or deposit with the commissioner a sum or sums required by those sections to be deducted, withheld, and paid, is personally and individually liable to the state for the sum or sums, and added penalties and interest, and is not liable to another person for that payment or payments. The sum or sums deducted and withheld under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.92#stat.290.92.2a">290.92, subdivision 2a</a> or 3, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.923#stat.290.923.2">290.923, subdivision 2</a>, must be held as a special fund in trust for the state of Minnesota.</p>
          <p>(b) If the employer or person withholding tax under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.92">290.92</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.923#stat.290.923.2">290.923, subdivision 2</a>, fails to deduct and withhold the tax in violation of those sections, and later the taxes against which the tax may be credited are paid, the tax required to be deducted and withheld will not be collected from the employer. This does not, however, relieve the employer from liability for any penalties and interest otherwise applicable for failure to deduct and withhold. This paragraph does not apply to an employer subject to paragraph (g).</p>
          <p>(c) Liability for payment of withholding taxes includes a responsible person or entity described in the personal liability provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.56">270C.56</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) Liability for payment of withholding taxes includes a third-party lender or surety described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.59">270C.59</a>.</p>
          <p>(e) A partnership or S corporation required to withhold and remit tax under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.92">290.92</a>, subdivisions 4b and 4c, is liable for payment of the tax to the commissioner, and a person having control of or responsibility for the withholding of the tax or the filing of returns due in connection with the tax is personally liable for the tax due.</p>
          <p>(f) A payor of sums required to be withheld under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.9705#stat.290.9705.1">290.9705, subdivision 1</a>, is liable to the state for the amount required to be deducted, and is not liable to an out-of-state contractor for the amount of the payment.</p>
          <p>(g) If an employer fails to withhold tax from the wages of an employee when required to do so under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.92#stat.290.92.2a">290.92, subdivision 2a</a>, by reason of treating such employee as not being an employee, then the liability for tax is equal to three percent of the wages paid to the employee. The liability for tax of an employee is not affected by the assessment or collection of tax under this paragraph. The employer is not entitled to recover from the employee any tax determined under this paragraph.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.31.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.117.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 117</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.31.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Sales and use tax.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The sales and use tax required to be collected by the retailer under chapter 297A constitutes a debt owed by the retailer to Minnesota, and the sums collected must be held as a special fund in trust for the state of Minnesota.</p>
          <p>A retailer who does not maintain a place of business within this state as defined by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.66#stat.297A.66.1">297A.66, subdivision 1</a>, shall not be indebted to Minnesota for amounts of tax that it was required to collect but did not collect unless the retailer knew or had been advised by the commissioner of its obligation to collect the tax.</p>
          <p>(b) The use tax required to be paid by a purchaser is a debt owed by the purchaser to Minnesota.</p>
          <p>(c) The tax imposed by chapter 297A, and interest and penalties, is a personal debt of the individual required to file a return from the time the liability arises, irrespective of when the time for payment of that liability occurs. The debt is, in the case of the executor or administrator of the estate of a decedent and in the case of a fiduciary, that of the individual in an official or fiduciary capacity unless the individual has voluntarily distributed the assets held in that capacity without reserving sufficient assets to pay the tax, interest, and penalties, in which case the individual is personally liable for the deficiency.</p>
          <p>(d) Liability for payment of sales and use taxes includes any responsible person or entity described in the personal liability provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.56">270C.56</a>.</p>
          <p>(e) Any amounts collected, even if erroneously or illegally collected, from a purchaser under a representation that they are taxes imposed under chapter 297A are state funds from the time of collection and must be reported on a return filed with the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.31.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Liability of vendor for repayment of refund.</span></h2>
          <p>If an individual income tax refund resulting from claiming an education credit under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.0674">290.0674</a> is paid by means of directly depositing the proceeds of the refund into a bank account controlled by the vendor of the product or service upon which the education credit is based, and the commissioner subsequently disallows the credit, the commissioner may seek repayment of the refund from the vendor. The amount of the repayment must be assessed and collected in the same time and manner as an erroneous refund under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.37#stat.289A.37.2">289A.37, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.289A.31.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/480/#laws.1.14.0">1990 c 480 art 1 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/291/#laws.6.46.0">1991 c 291 art 6 s 46</a>; art 11 s 7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/511/#laws.6.19.0">1992 c 511 art 6 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/375/#laws.8.14.0">1993 c 375 art 8 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/587/#laws.1.24.0">1994 c 587 art 1 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/84/#laws.6.22.0">1997 c 84 art 6 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/243/#laws.16.14.0">1999 c 243 art 16 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/418/#laws.1.3.0">2000 c 418 art 1 s 3</a>,44; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.12.2.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 12 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/127/#laws.3.3.0">2003 c 127 art 3 s 3</a>; art 8 s 7,8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/21/#laws.8.3.0">1Sp2003 c 21 art 8 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.2.17.0">2005 c 151 art 2 s 17</a>; art 6 s 4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/88/#laws.7.5.0">2009 c 88 art 7 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/295/#laws.2.11.0">2012 c 295 art 2 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/1/#laws.13.7.0">1Sp2017 c 1 art 13 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/6/#laws.2.9.0">1Sp2019 c 6 art 2 s 9</a>; art 24 s 8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/14/#laws.2.2.0">1Sp2021 c 14 art 2 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.289A.33">
        <h1 class="shn">289A.33 FILING REQUIREMENTS AND DUE DATES; SPECIAL RULES.</h1>
        <p>(a) Upon the request of any cannabis business as defined by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/342.01">342.01, subdivision </a>14, required to collect and remit taxes imposed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/295.81">295.81</a>, chapter 290, or chapter 297A, the commissioner shall waive the requirement that payment of tax must be made electronically if the failure to pay electronically is because the cannabis business is unable to secure banking services and the inability to secure the services is due to its engagement in cannabis-related business allowed under Minnesota law.</p>
        <p>(b) If, in consultation with the commissioner of commerce, the commissioner determines that the inability to find banking services is widespread and enforcement of the electronic payment requirement will significantly impede the ability of cannabis businesses to timely pay taxes imposed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/295.81">295.81</a>, chapter 290, or chapter 297A, the commissioner may publish notice on the department website that waives the requirement to pay the tax electronically. If such notice is published, a cannabis business must file returns and pay taxes lawfully due in the form and manner prescribed by the commissioner.</p>
        <p>(c) Nothing in this section relieves a cannabis business from timely filing and paying taxes.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.289A.33.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/63/#laws.2.6.0">2023 c 63 art 2 s 6</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">ASSESSMENTS, EXAMINATIONS,<br>AND STATUTES OF LIMITATIONS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.289A.35">
        <h1 class="shn">289A.35 ASSESSMENTS ON RETURNS.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner may audit and adjust the taxpayer's computation of federal adjusted gross income, federal taxable income, items of federal tax preferences, or federal credit amounts to make them conform with the provisions of chapter 290 or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/298.01">298.01</a>. If a return has been filed, the commissioner shall enter the liability reported on the return and may make any audit or investigation that is considered necessary.</p>
        <p>(b) Upon petition by a taxpayer, and when the commissioner determines that it is in the best interest of the state, the commissioner may allow S corporations and partnerships to receive orders of assessment issued under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.33#stat.270C.33.4">270C.33, subdivision 4</a>, on behalf of their owners, and to pay liabilities shown on such orders. In such cases, the owners' liability must be calculated using the method provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.08#stat.289A.08.7">289A.08, subdivision 7</a>, paragraph (b).</p>
        <p>(c) A taxpayer may petition the commissioner for the use of the method described in paragraph (b) after the taxpayer is notified that an audit has been initiated and before an order of assessment has been issued.</p>
        <p>(d) A determination of the commissioner under paragraph (b) to grant or deny the petition of a taxpayer cannot be appealed to the Tax Court or any other court.</p>
        <p>(e) The commissioner may audit and adjust the taxpayer's computation of tax under chapter 291. In the case of a return filed pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.10">289A.10</a>, the commissioner shall notify the estate no later than nine months after the filing date, as provided by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.38#stat.289A.38.2">289A.38, subdivision 2</a>, whether the return is under examination or the return has been processed as filed.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.289A.35.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/480/#laws.1.15.0">1990 c 480 art 1 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/291/#laws.11.8.0">1991 c 291 art 11 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/31/#laws.2.5.0">1997 c 31 art 2 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/490/#laws.13.11.0">2000 c 490 art 13 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.2.8.0">2005 c 151 art 2 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/112/#laws.2.2.0">2011 c 112 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/1/#laws.13.8.0">1Sp2017 c 1 art 13 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/6/#laws.1.7.0">1Sp2019 c 6 art 1 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.289A.36"><b>289A.36</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.117.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 117</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.289A.37">
        <h1 class="shn">289A.37 ASSESSMENTS; ERRONEOUS REFUNDS; JOINT INCOME TAX RETURNS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.37.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.117.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 117</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.37.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Erroneous refunds.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except as provided in paragraph (b), an erroneous refund occurs when the commissioner issues a payment to a person that exceeds the amount the person is entitled to receive under law. An erroneous refund is considered an underpayment of tax on the date issued.</p>
          <p>(b) To the extent that the amount paid does not exceed the amount claimed by the taxpayer, an erroneous refund does not include the following:</p>
          <p>(1) any amount of a refund or credit paid pursuant to a claim for refund filed by a taxpayer, including but not limited to refunds of claims made under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.06">290.06</a>, subdivision 23; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.067">290.067</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.0671">290.0671</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.0672">290.0672</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.0674">290.0674</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.0675">290.0675</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.0677">290.0677</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.068">290.068</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.0681">290.0681</a>; or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.0692">290.0692</a>; or chapter 290A; or</p>
          <p>(2) any amount paid pursuant to a claim for refund of an overpayment of tax filed by a taxpayer.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner may make an assessment to recover an erroneous refund at any time within two years from the issuance of the erroneous refund. If all or part of the erroneous refund was induced by fraud or misrepresentation of a material fact, the assessment may be made at any time.</p>
          <p>(d) Assessments of amounts that are not erroneous refunds under paragraph (b) must be conducted under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.38">289A.38</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.382">289A.382</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.37.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.117.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 117</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.37.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.117.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 117</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.37.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.117.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 117</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.37.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Order of assessment if joint income tax return.</span></h2>
          <p>If a joint income tax return is filed by spouses, an order of assessment may be a single joint notice. If the commissioner has been notified by either spouse that that spouse's address has changed and if that spouse requests it, then, instead of the single joint notice mailed to the last known address of the spouses, a duplicate or original of the joint notice must be sent to the requesting spouse at the address designated by the requesting spouse. The other joint notice must be mailed to the other spouse at that spouse's last known address. An assessment is not invalid for failure to send it to a spouse if the spouse actually receives the notice in the same period as if it had been mailed to that spouse at the correct address or if the spouse has failed to provide an address to the commissioner other than the last known address.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.289A.37.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/480/#laws.1.17.0">1990 c 480 art 1 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/291/#laws.16.8.0">1991 c 291 art 16 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/511/#laws.6.10.0">1992 c 511 art 6 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/510/#laws.4.9.0">1994 c 510 art 4 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/84/#laws.6.23.0">1997 c 84 art 6 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/1/#laws.16.27.0">1Sp2017 c 1 art 16 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/6/#laws.24.9.0">1Sp2019 c 6 art 24 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/14/#laws.2.3.0">1Sp2021 c 14 art 2 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.289A.38">
        <h1 class="shn">289A.38 LIMITATIONS ON TIME FOR ASSESSMENT OF TAX.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.38.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">General rule.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as otherwise provided in this section, the amount of taxes assessable must be assessed within 3-1/2 years after the date the return is filed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.38.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Filing date.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section, a tax return filed before the last day prescribed by law for filing is considered to be filed on the last day.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.38.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/112/#laws.2.5.0">2011 c 112 art 2 s 5</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.38.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Property tax refund.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of computing the limitation under this section, the due date of the property tax refund return as provided for in chapter 290A is the due date for an income tax return covering the year preceding the year in which the property taxes are payable.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.38.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">False or fraudulent return; no return.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding the limitations under subdivision 1, the tax may be assessed at any time if a false or fraudulent return is filed or when a taxpayer fails to file a return.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.38.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Omission in excess of 25 percent.</span></h2>
          <p>Additional taxes may be assessed within 6-1/2 years after the due date of the return or the date the return was filed, whichever is later, if:</p>
          <p>(1) the taxpayer omits from gross income an amount properly includable in it that is in excess of 25 percent of the amount of gross income stated in the return;</p>
          <p>(2) the taxpayer omits from a sales, use, or withholding tax return, or a return for a tax imposed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/295.52">295.52</a>, an amount of taxes in excess of 25 percent of the taxes reported in the return; or</p>
          <p>(3) the taxpayer omits from the gross estate assets in excess of 25 percent of the gross estate reported in the return.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.38.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Federal tax changes.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If the amount of income, items of tax preference, deductions, or credits for any year of a taxpayer, or the wages paid by a taxpayer for any period, as reported to the Internal Revenue Service is changed or corrected by the commissioner of Internal Revenue or other officer of the United States or other competent authority, or where a renegotiation of a contract or subcontract with the United States results in a change in income, items of tax preference, deductions, credits, or withholding tax, or, in the case of estate tax, where there are adjustments to the taxable estate, the taxpayer shall report the federal adjustments in writing to the commissioner. The federal adjustments report must be submitted within 180 days after the final determination date and must be in the form of either an amended Minnesota estate, withholding tax, corporate franchise tax, or income tax return conceding the accuracy of the federal adjustment or a letter detailing how the federal adjustment is incorrect or does not change the Minnesota tax. An amended Minnesota income tax return must be accompanied by an amended property tax refund return, if necessary. A taxpayer filing an amended federal tax return must also file a copy of the amended return with the commissioner of revenue within 180 days after filing the amended return.</p>
          <p>(b) In the case of a final federal adjustment arising from a partnership-level audit or an administrative adjustment request filed by a partnership under section 6227 of the Internal Revenue Code, a taxpayer must report adjustments as provided for under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.382">289A.382</a>, and not this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.38.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Failure to report change or correction of federal return.</span></h2>
          <p>If a taxpayer fails to make a federal adjustments report as required by subdivision 7 or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.382">289A.382</a>, the commissioner may recompute the tax, including a refund, based on information available to the commissioner. The tax may be recomputed within six years after the federal adjustments report should have been filed, notwithstanding any period of limitations to the contrary.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.38.9">
