          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Report made of change or correction of federal return.</span></h2>
          <p>If a taxpayer is required to make a federal adjustments report under subdivision 7 or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.382">289A.382</a>, and does report the change or files a copy of the amended return, the commissioner may recompute and reassess the tax due, including a refund (1) within one year after the federal adjustments report or amended return is filed with the commissioner, notwithstanding any period of limitations to the contrary, or (2) within any other applicable period stated in this section, whichever period is longer. The period provided for the carryback of any amount of loss or credit is also extended as provided in this subdivision, notwithstanding any law to the contrary. If the commissioner has completed a field audit of the taxpayer, and, but for this subdivision, the commissioner's time period to adjust the tax has expired, the additional tax due or refund is limited to only those changes that are required to be made to the return which relate to the changes made on the federal return. This subdivision does not apply to sales and use tax.</p>
          <p>For purposes of this subdivision and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.42#stat.289A.42.2">289A.42, subdivision 2</a>, a "field audit" is the physical presence of examiners in the taxpayer's or taxpayer's representative's office conducting an examination of the taxpayer with the intention of issuing an assessment or notice of change in tax or which results in the issuing of an assessment or notice of change in tax. The examination may include inspecting a taxpayer's place of business, tangible personal property, equipment, computer systems and facilities, pertinent books, records, papers, vouchers, computer printouts, accounts, and documents.</p>
          <p>A taxpayer may make estimated payments to the commissioner of the tax expected to result from a pending audit by the Internal Revenue Service. The taxpayer may make estimated payments prior to the due date of the federal adjustments report without the taxpayer having to file the report with the commissioner. The commissioner must credit the estimated tax payments against any tax liability of the taxpayer ultimately found to be due to the commissioner. The estimated payments limit the accrual of further statutory interest on that amount. If the estimated tax payments exceed the final tax liability plus statutory interest ultimately determined to be due, the taxpayer is entitled to a refund or credit for the excess, provided the taxpayer files a federal adjustments report, or claim for refund or credit of tax, no later than one year following the final determination date.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.38.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Incorrect determination of federal adjusted gross income.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding any other provision of this chapter, if a taxpayer whose net income is determined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.01#stat.290.01.19">290.01, subdivision 19</a>, omits from income an amount that will under the Internal Revenue Code extend the statute of limitations for the assessment of federal income taxes, or otherwise incorrectly determines the taxpayer's federal adjusted gross income resulting in adjustments by the Internal Revenue Service, then the period of assessment and determination of tax will be that under the Internal Revenue Code. When a change is made to federal income during the extended time provided under this subdivision, the provisions under subdivisions 7 to 9 and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.382">289A.382</a> regarding additional extensions apply.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.38.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Net operating loss carryback.</span></h2>
          <p>If a deficiency of tax is attributable to a net operating loss carryback that has been disallowed in whole or in part, the deficiency may be assessed at any time that a deficiency for the taxable year of the loss may be assessed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.38.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Request for early audit for individual income, fiduciary income, mining company, and corporate franchise taxes.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Tax must be assessed within 18 months after written request for an assessment has been made in the case of income received (1) during the lifetime of a decedent, (2) by the decedent's estate during the period of administration, (3) by a trustee of a terminating trust or other fiduciary who, because of custody of assets, would be liable for the payment of tax under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.58#stat.270C.58.2">270C.58, subdivision 2</a>, or (4) by a mining company or a corporation. A proceeding in court for the collection of the tax must begin within two years after written request for the assessment (filed after the return is made and in the form the commissioner prescribes) by the personal representative or other fiduciary representing the estate of the decedent, or by the trustee of a terminating trust or other fiduciary who, because of custody of assets, would be liable for the payment of tax under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.58#stat.270C.58.2">270C.58, subdivision 2</a>, or by the corporation. Except as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.42#stat.289A.42.1">289A.42, subdivision 1</a>, an assessment must not be made after the expiration of 3-1/2 years after the return was filed, and an action must not be brought after the expiration of four years after the return was filed.</p>
          <p>(b) Paragraph (a) only applies in the case of a mining company or a corporation if:</p>
          <p>(1) the written request notifies the commissioner that the corporation contemplates dissolution at or before the expiration of the 18-month period;</p>
          <p>(2) the dissolution is begun in good faith before the expiration of the 18-month period; and</p>
          <p>(3) the dissolution is completed within the 18-month period.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.38.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.117.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 117</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.38.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Failure to timely file withholding reconciliation.</span></h2>
          <p>If an employer fails to timely file the reconciliation required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.09#stat.289A.09.2">289A.09, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (d), withholding taxes may be assessed within the period prescribed in subdivision 1, or within one year from the date the reconciliation is filed with the commissioner, whichever is later.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.38.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.<span class="headnote">Purchaser filed refund claims.</span></h2>
          <p>If a purchaser refund claim is filed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.50#stat.289A.50.2a">289A.50, subdivision 2a</a>, and the basis for the claim is that the purchaser was improperly charged tax on an improvement to real property or on the purchase of nontaxable services, sales or use tax may be assessed for the cost of materials used to make the real property improvement or to perform the nontaxable service. The assessment may be made against the person making the improvement to real property or the sale of nontaxable services, within the period prescribed in subdivision 1, or within one year after the date of the refund order, whichever is later.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.38.16">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 16.<span class="headnote">Reportable transactions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If a taxpayer fails to include on any return or statement for any taxable year any information with respect to a reportable transaction, as required by federal law and under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.121">289A.121</a>, the commissioner may recompute the tax, including a refund, within the later of:</p>
          <p>(1) six years after the return is filed with respect to the taxable year in which the taxpayer participated in the reportable transaction; or</p>
          <p>(2) for a listed transaction, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.121">289A.121</a>, for which the taxpayer fails to include on any return or statement for any taxable year any information that is required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.121">289A.121</a>, one year after the earlier of:</p>
          <p>(i) the date the taxpayer furnishes the required information to the commissioner; or</p>
          <p>(ii) the date that a material advisor, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.121">289A.121</a>, meets the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.121">289A.121</a>, relating to the transaction with respect to the taxpayer.</p>
          <p>(b) If tax is assessable solely because of this section, the assessable deficiency is limited to the items that were not disclosed as required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.121">289A.121</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.289A.38.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/480/#laws.1.18.0">1990 c 480 art 1 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/291/#laws.6.12.0">1991 c 291 art 6 s 12</a>,13,46; art 11 s 9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/511/#laws.6.19.0">1992 c 511 art 6 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/375/#laws.8.14.0">1993 c 375 art 8 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/587/#laws.1.24.0">1994 c 587 art 1 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/264/#laws.10.3.0">1995 c 264 art 10 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/31/#laws.1.11.0">1997 c 31 art 1 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/300/#laws.1.3.0">1998 c 300 art 1 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.2.17.0">2005 c 151 art 2 s 17</a>; art 6 s 5; art 7 s 1,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/3/#laws.8.3.0">1Sp2005 c 3 art 8 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/154/#laws.11.6.0">2008 c 154 art 11 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/88/#laws.7.6.0">2009 c 88 art 7 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/112/#laws.2.3.0">2011 c 112 art 2 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/1/#laws.14.2.0">1Sp2017 c 1 art 14 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/6/#laws.13.1.0">1Sp2019 c 6 art 13 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/14/#laws.2.4.0">1Sp2021 c 14 art 2 s 4</a>-7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/64/#laws.7.5.0">2023 c 64 art 7 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.289A.381">
        <h1 class="shn">289A.381 DEFINITIONS; PARTNERSHIPS; FEDERAL ADJUSTMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.381.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions relating to federal adjustments.</span></h2>
          <p>Unless otherwise specified, the definitions in this section apply for the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.38">289A.38, subdivisions 7</a> to 9, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.381">289A.381</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.382">289A.382</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.381.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Administrative adjustment request.</span></h2>
          <p>"Administrative adjustment request" means an administrative adjustment request filed by a partnership under section 6227 of the Internal Revenue Code.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.381.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Audited partnership.</span></h2>
          <p>"Audited partnership" means a partnership subject to a federal adjustment resulting from a partnership-level audit.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.381.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Corporate partner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Corporate partner" means a partner that is subject to tax under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.02">290.02</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.381.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Direct partner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Direct partner" means a partner that holds an immediate legal ownership interest in a partnership or pass-through entity.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.381.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Exempt partner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Exempt partner" means a partner that is exempt from taxes on its net income under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.05#stat.290.05.1">290.05, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.381.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Federal adjustment.</span></h2>
          <p>"Federal adjustment" means any change in an amount calculated under the Internal Revenue Code, whether to income, gross estate, a credit, an item of preference, or any other item that is used by a taxpayer to compute a tax administered under this chapter for the reviewed year whether that change results from action by the Internal Revenue Service or other competent authority, including a partnership-level audit, or from the filing of an amended federal return, federal refund claim, or an administrative adjustment request by the taxpayer. A federal adjustment is positive to the extent that it increases taxable income as determined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.01#stat.290.01.29">290.01, subdivision 29</a>, and is negative to the extent that it decreases taxable income as determined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.01">290.01, subdivision </a>29.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.381.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Federal adjustments report.</span></h2>
          <p>"Federal adjustments report" includes a method or form prescribed by the commissioner for use by a taxpayer to report federal adjustments, including an amended Minnesota tax return or a uniform multistate report.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.381.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Federal partnership representative.</span></h2>
          <p>"Federal partnership representative" means the person the partnership designates for the taxable year as the partnership's representative, or the person the Internal Revenue Service has appointed to act as the partnership representative, pursuant to section 6223(a) of the Internal Revenue Code.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.381.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Final determination date.</span></h2>
          <p>"Final determination date" means:</p>
          <p>(1) for a federal adjustment arising from an audit by the Internal Revenue Service or other competent authority, the first day on which no federal adjustment arising from that audit remains to be finally determined, whether by agreement, or, if appealed or contested, by a final decision with respect to which all rights of appeal have been waived or exhausted;</p>
          <p>(2) for a federal adjustment arising from an audit or other action by the Internal Revenue Service or other competent authority, if the taxpayer filed as a member of a combined report under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.17#stat.290.17.4">290.17, subdivision 4</a>, the first day on which no related federal adjustments arising from that audit remain to be finally determined as described in clause (1) for the entire combined group;</p>
          <p>(3) for a federal adjustment arising from the filing of an amended federal return, a federal refund claim, or the filing by a partnership of an administrative adjustment request, the date on which the amended return, refund claim, or administrative adjustment request was filed; or</p>
          <p>(4) for agreements required to be signed by the Internal Revenue Service and the taxpayer, the date on which the last party signed the agreement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.381.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Final federal adjustment.</span></h2>
          <p>"Final federal adjustment" means a federal adjustment after the final determination date for that federal adjustment has passed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.381.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Indirect partner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Indirect partner" means either:</p>
          <p>(1) a partner in a partnership or pass-through entity that itself holds an immediate legal ownership interest in another partnership or pass-through entity; or</p>
          <p>(2) a partner in a partnership or pass-through entity that holds an indirect interest in another partnership or pass-through entity through another indirect partner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.381.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Partner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Partner" means a person that holds an interest directly or indirectly in a partnership or other pass-through entity.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.381.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Partnership.</span></h2>
          <p>"Partnership" has the meaning provided under section 7701(a)(2) of the Internal Revenue Code.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.381.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.<span class="headnote">Partnership-level audit.</span></h2>
          <p>"Partnership-level audit" means an examination by the Internal Revenue Service at the partnership level pursuant to subtitle F, chapter 63, subchapter C, of the Internal Revenue Code, which results in federal adjustments and adjustments to partnership-related items.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.381.16">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 16.<span class="headnote">Pass-through entity.</span></h2>
          <p>"Pass-through entity" means an entity, other than a partnership, that is not subject to the tax imposed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.02">290.02</a>. The term pass-through entity includes but is not limited to S corporations, estates, and trusts other than grantor trusts.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.381.17">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 17.<span class="headnote">Resident partner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Resident partner" means an individual, trust, or estate partner who is a resident of Minnesota under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.01#stat.290.01.7">290.01, subdivision 7</a>, 7a, or 7b, for the relevant tax period.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.381.18">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 18.<span class="headnote">Reviewed year.</span></h2>
          <p>"Reviewed year" means the taxable year of a partnership that is subject to a partnership-level audit from which federal adjustments arise.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.381.19">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 19.<span class="headnote">Tiered partner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Tiered partner" means any partner that is a partnership or pass-through entity.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.381.20">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 20.<span class="headnote">Unrelated business taxable income.</span></h2>
          <p>"Unrelated business taxable income" has the meaning provided under section 512 of the Internal Revenue Code.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.289A.381.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/14/#laws.2.8.0">1Sp2021 c 14 art 2 s 8</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.289A.382">
        <h1 class="shn">289A.382 REPORTING AND PAYMENT REQUIREMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.382.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">State partnership representative.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) With respect to an action required or permitted to be taken by a partnership under this section, or in a proceeding under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.35">270C.35</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/271.06">271.06</a>, the state partnership representative for the reviewed year shall have the sole authority to act on behalf of the partnership, and its direct partners and indirect partners shall be bound by those actions.</p>
          <p>(b) The state partnership representative for the reviewed year is the partnership's federal partnership representative unless the partnership, in a form and manner prescribed by the commissioner, designates another person as its state partnership representative.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.382.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Reporting and payment requirements for partnerships and tiered partners.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except for when an audited partnership makes the election in subdivision 3, and except for negative federal adjustments required under federal law taken into account by the partnership in the partnership return for the adjustment or other year, all final federal adjustments of an audited partnership must comply with paragraph (b) and each direct partner of the audited partnership, other than a tiered partner, must comply with paragraph (c).</p>
          <p>(b) No later than 90 days after the final determination date, the audited partnership must:</p>
          <p>(1) file a completed federal adjustments report, including all partner-level information required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.12#stat.289A.12.3">289A.12, subdivision 3</a>, with the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(2) notify each of its direct partners of their distributive share of the final federal adjustments;</p>
          <p>(3) file an amended composite report for all direct partners who were included in a composite return under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.08#stat.289A.08.7">289A.08, subdivision 7</a>, in the reviewed year, and pay the additional amount that would have been due had the federal adjustments been reported properly as required;</p>
          <p>(4) file amended withholding reports for all direct partners who were or should have been subject to nonresident withholding under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.92#stat.290.92.4b">290.92, subdivision 4b</a>, in the reviewed year, and pay the additional amount that would have been due had the federal adjustments been reported properly as required; and</p>
          <p>(5) file an amended pass-through entity tax report for all direct partners who were included in a pass-through entity tax return under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.08#stat.289A.08.7a">289A.08, subdivision 7a</a>, in the reviewed year, and pay the additional amount that would have been due had the federal adjustments been reported properly as required.</p>
          <p>(c) No later than 180 days after the final determination date, each direct partner, other than a tiered partner, that is subject to a tax administered under this chapter, other than the sales tax, must:</p>
          <p>(1) file a federal adjustments report reporting their distributive share of the adjustments reported to them under paragraph (b), clause (2); and</p>
          <p>(2) pay any additional amount of tax due as if the final federal adjustment had been properly reported, plus any penalty and interest due under this chapter, and less any credit for related amounts paid or withheld and remitted on behalf of the direct partner under paragraph (b), clauses (3) and (4).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.382.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Election; partnership or tiered partners pay.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An audited partnership may make an election under this subdivision to pay its assessment at the entity level. If an audited partnership makes an election to pay its assessment at the entity level, it must:</p>
          <p>(1) no later than 90 days after the final determination date:</p>
          <p>(i) file a completed federal adjustments report, which includes the residency information for all individual, trust, and estate direct partners and information pertaining to all other direct partners as prescribed by the commissioner; and</p>
          <p>(ii) notify the commissioner that it is making the election under this subdivision; and</p>
          <p>(2) no later than 180 days after the final determination date, pay an amount, determined as follows, in lieu of taxes on partners:</p>
          <p>(i) exclude from final federal adjustments the distributive share of these adjustments made to a direct exempt partner that is not unrelated business taxable income;</p>
          <p>(ii) exclude from final federal adjustments the distributive share of these adjustments made to a direct partner that has filed a federal adjustments report and paid the applicable tax, as required under subdivision 2, for the distributive share of adjustments reported on a federal return under section 6225(c) of the Internal Revenue Code;</p>
          <p>(iii) assign and apportion at the partnership level using sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.17">290.17</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.20">290.20</a> the total distributive share of the remaining final federal adjustments for the reviewed year attributed to direct corporate partners and direct exempt partners; multiply the total by the highest tax rate in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.06#stat.290.06.1">290.06, subdivision 1</a>, for the reviewed year; and calculate interest and penalties as applicable under this chapter;</p>
          <p>(iv) allocate at the partnership level using section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.17#stat.290.17.1">290.17, subdivision 1</a>, the total distributive share of all final federal adjustments attributable to individual resident direct partners for the reviewed year; multiply the total by the highest tax rate in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.06">290.06</a>, subdivision 2c, for the reviewed year; and calculate interest and penalties as applicable under this chapter;</p>
          <p>(v) assign and apportion at the partnership level using sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.17">290.17</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.20">290.20</a> the total distributive share of the remaining final federal adjustments attributable to nonresident individual direct partners and direct partners who are an estate or a trust for the reviewed year; multiply the total by the highest tax rate in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.06#stat.290.06.2c">290.06, subdivision 2c</a>, for the reviewed year; and calculate interest and penalties as applicable under this chapter;</p>
          <p>(vi) for the total distributive share of the remaining final federal adjustments reported to tiered partners:</p>
          <p>(A) determine the amount of the adjustments that would be assigned using section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.17">290.17</a>, subdivision 2, paragraphs (a) to (d), excluding income or gains from intangible personal property not employed in the business of the recipient of the income or gains if the recipient of the income or gains is a resident of this state or is a resident trust or estate under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.17#stat.290.17.2">290.17, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (c), or apportioned using sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.17#stat.290.17.3">290.17, subdivision 3</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.191">290.191</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.20">290.20</a>; and then determine the portion of the amount that would be allocated to this state;</p>
          <p>(B) determine the amount of the adjustments that are fully sourced to the taxpayer's state of residency under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.17#stat.290.17.2">290.17, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (e), and income or gains from intangible personal property not employed in the business of the recipient of the income or gains if the recipient of the income or gains is a resident of this state or is a resident trust or estate under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.17#stat.290.17.2">290.17, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (c);</p>
          <p>(C) determine the portion of the amount determined in subitem (B) that can be established to be properly allocable to nonresident indirect partners or other partners not subject to tax on the adjustments; and</p>
          <p>(D) multiply the total of the amounts determined in subitems (A) and (B) reduced by the amount determined in subitem (C) by the highest tax rate in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.06">290.06, subdivision </a>2c, for the reviewed year, and calculate interest and penalties as applicable under this chapter; and</p>
          <p>(vii) add the amounts determined in items (iii) to (vi), and pay all applicable taxes, penalties, and interest to the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) An audited partnership may not make an election under this subdivision to report:</p>
          <p>(1) a federal adjustment that results in unitary business income to a corporate partner required to file as a member of a combined report under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.17#stat.290.17.4">290.17, subdivision 4</a>; or</p>
          <p>(2) any final federal adjustments resulting from an administrative adjustment request.</p>
          <p>(c) An audited partnership not otherwise subject to any reporting or payment obligation to this state may not make an election under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.382.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Tiered partners and indirect partners.</span></h2>
          <p>The direct and indirect partners of an audited partnership that are tiered partners, and all the partners of the tiered partners, that are subject to tax under chapter 290 are subject to the reporting and payment requirements contained in subdivision 2, and the tiered partners are entitled to make the elections provided in subdivision 3. The tiered partners or their partners shall make required reports and payments no later than 90 days after the time for filing and furnishing of statements to tiered partners and their partners as established under section 6226 of the Internal Revenue Code.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.382.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Effects of election by partnership or tiered partner and payment of amount due.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Unless the commissioner determines otherwise, an election under subdivision 3 is irrevocable.</p>
          <p>(b) If an audited partnership or tiered partner properly reports and pays an amount determined in subdivision 3, the amount must be treated as paid in lieu of taxes owed by the partnership's direct partners and indirect partners, to the extent applicable, on the same final federal adjustments. The direct partners or indirect partners of the partnership who are not resident partners may not take any deduction or credit for this amount or claim a refund of the amount in this state.</p>
          <p>(c) Nothing in this subdivision precludes resident direct partners from claiming a credit against taxes paid under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.06">290.06</a> on any amounts paid by the audited partnership or tiered partners on the resident partner's behalf to another state or local tax jurisdiction.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.382.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Failure of partnership or tiered partner to report or pay.</span></h2>
          <p>Nothing in this section prevents the commissioner from assessing direct partners or indirect partners for taxes they owe, using the best information available, in the event that, for any reason, a partnership or tiered partner fails to timely make any report or payment required by this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.289A.382.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/14/#laws.2.9.0">1Sp2021 c 14 art 2 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/64/#laws.1.15.0">2023 c 64 art 1 s 15</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.289A.39">
        <h1 class="shn">289A.39 LIMITATIONS; ARMED SERVICES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.39.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Extensions for service members.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The limitations of time provided by this chapter, chapter 290 relating to income taxes, chapter 271 relating to the Tax Court for filing returns, paying taxes, claiming refunds, commencing action thereon, appealing to the Tax Court from orders relating to income taxes, and the filing of petitions under chapter 278 that would otherwise be due prior to May 1 of the year in which the taxes are payable, and appealing to the supreme court from decisions of the Tax Court relating to income taxes are extended, as provided in section 7508 of the Internal Revenue Code.</p>
          <p>(b) If a member of the National Guard or reserves is called to active duty in the armed forces, the limitations of time provided by this chapter and chapters 290 and 290A relating to income taxes and claims for property tax refunds are extended by the following period of time:</p>
          <p>(1) in the case of an individual whose active service is in the United States, six months; or</p>
          <p>(2) in the case of an individual whose active service includes service abroad, the period of initial service plus six months.</p>
          <p>Nothing in this paragraph reduces the time within which an act is required or permitted under paragraph (a).</p>
          <p>(c) If an individual entitled to the benefit of paragraph (a) files a return during the period disregarded under paragraph (a), interest must be paid on an overpayment or refundable credit from the due date of the return, notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.56#stat.289A.56.2">289A.56, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) The provisions of this subdivision apply to the spouse of an individual entitled to the benefits of this subdivision with respect to a joint return filed by the spouses.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.39.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Interest and penalties.</span></h2>
          <p>Interest on income tax must not be assessed or collected from an individual, and interest must not be paid upon an income tax refund to any individual, with respect to whom, and for the period during which, the limitations or time are extended as provided in subdivision 1. A penalty will not be assessed or collected from an individual for failure during that period to perform an act required by the laws described in subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.39.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Assessments; actions.</span></h2>
          <p>The time limitations provided for the assessment of a tax, penalty, or interest, are extended, with respect to those individuals and for the period provided in subdivision 1 and for a further period of six months; and the time limitations for the commencement of action to collect a tax, penalty, or interest from those individuals are extended for a period ending six months after the expiration of the time for assessment as provided in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.39.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>Nothing in this section reduces the time within which an act is required or permitted under this chapter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.39.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Extension limitations.</span></h2>
          <p>This section does not extend the time for performing any of the acts set forth in this chapter beyond the expiration of three months after the appointment of a personal representative or guardian, in this state, for any individual described in this section, except as provided in subdivision 6.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.39.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Death while serving in armed forces.</span></h2>
          <p>If an individual dies while in active service as a member of the military or naval forces of the United States or of any of the United Nations, an income tax imposed under chapter 290 will not be imposed for the taxable year in which the individual dies. Income tax imposed for a prior taxable year that is unpaid at the date of death (including additions to the tax, penalties) must not be assessed, and if assessed, the assessment must be abated. In addition, upon the filing of a claim for refund within seven years from the date the return was filed, the tax paid or collected with respect to any taxable year beginning after December 31, 1949, during which the decedent was in active service must be refunded.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.39.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Death of civilian while outside United States.</span></h2>
          <p>If an individual dies while a civilian employee of the United States as a result of wounds or injuries incurred while the individual was a civilian employee of the United States, and which were incurred outside the United States in a terroristic or military action, a tax imposed by chapter 290 does not apply with respect to the taxable year in which the death falls and with respect to any prior taxable years in the period beginning with the last taxable year ending before the taxable year in which the wounds or injury were incurred. Terroristic or military action has the meaning given it in section 692(c)(2) of the Internal Revenue Code.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.289A.39.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/480/#laws.1.19.0">1990 c 480 art 1 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/18/#laws.0.2.0">1991 c 18 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/291/#laws.6.46.0">1991 c 291 art 6 s 46</a>; art 21 s 12; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/511/#laws.6.19.0">1992 c 511 art 6 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/375/#laws.8.14.0">1993 c 375 art 8 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/587/#laws.1.24.0">1994 c 587 art 1 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/471/#laws.4.2.0">1996 c 471 art 4 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.6.6.0">2005 c 151 art 6 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.289A.40">
        <h1 class="shn">289A.40 LIMITATIONS ON CLAIMS FOR REFUND.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.40.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Time limit; generally.</span></h2>
          <p>Unless otherwise provided in this chapter, a claim for a refund of an overpayment of state tax must be filed within 3-1/2 years from the date prescribed for filing the return, plus any extension of time granted for filing the return, but only if filed within the extended time, or one year from the date of an order assessing tax under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.33">270C.33</a> or an order determining an appeal under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.35#stat.270C.35.8">270C.35, subdivision 8</a>, or one year from the date of a return made by the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.33#stat.270C.33.3">270C.33, subdivision 3</a>, upon payment in full of the tax, penalties, and interest shown on the order or return made by the commissioner, whichever period expires later. Claims for refund, except for taxes under chapter 297A, filed after the 3-1/2 year period but within the one-year period are limited to the amount of the tax, penalties, and interest on the order or return made by the commissioner and to issues determined by the order or return made by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>In the case of assessments under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.38#stat.289A.38.5">289A.38, subdivision 5</a> or 6, claims for refund under chapter 297A filed after the 3-1/2 year period but within the one-year period are limited to the amount of the tax, penalties, and interest on the order or return made by the commissioner that are due for the period before the 3-1/2 year period.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.40.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Individual income taxes; suspension during period of disability.</span></h2>
          <p>If the taxpayer meets the requirements for suspending the running of the time period to file a claim for refund under section 6511(h) of the Internal Revenue Code, the time period in subdivision 1 for the taxpayer to file a claim for an individual income tax refund is suspended.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.40.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Bad debt loss.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">If a claim relates to an overpayment because of a failure to deduct a loss due to a bad debt or to a security becoming worthless, the claim is considered timely if filed within seven years from the date prescribed for the filing of the return. A claim relating to an overpayment of taxes under chapter 297A must be filed within 3-1/2 years from the date when the bad debt was (1) written off as uncollectible in the taxpayer's books and records, and (2) either eligible to be deducted for federal income tax purposes or would have been eligible for a bad debt deduction for federal income tax purposes if the taxpayer were required to file a federal income tax return, or within one year from the date the taxpayer's federal income tax return is timely filed claiming the bad debt deduction, whichever period is later. The refund or credit is limited to the amount of overpayment attributable to the loss. "Bad debt" for purposes of this subdivision, has the same meaning as that term is used in United States Code, title 26, section 166, except that for a claim relating to an overpayment of taxes under chapter 297A the following are excluded from the calculation of bad debt: financing charges or interest; sales or use taxes charged on the purchase price; uncollectible amounts on property that remain in the possession of the seller until the full purchase price is paid; expenses incurred in attempting to collect any debt; and repossessed property. For purposes of reporting a payment received on previously claimed bad debt under chapter 297A, any payments made on a debt or account are applied first proportionally to the taxable price of the property or service and the sales tax on it, and secondly to interest, service charges, and any other charges.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.40.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Net operating loss; individuals.</span></h2>
          <p>A refund or credit must be allowed for a net operating loss carryback to any taxable year authorized by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.095">290.095</a>, or section 172 of the Internal Revenue Code, but the refund or credit is limited to the amount of overpayment arising from the carryback.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.40.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Property tax refund claims.</span></h2>
          <p>A property tax refund claim under chapter 290A is not allowed if the initial claim is filed more than one year after the original due date for filing the claim.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.40.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Purchaser filed refund claims.</span></h2>
          <p>A claim for refund of taxes paid on a transaction not subject to tax under chapter 297A, where the purchaser may apply directly to the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.50#stat.289A.50.2a">289A.50, subdivision 2a</a>, must be filed within 3-1/2 years from the 20th day of the month following the month of the invoice date for the purchase.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.40.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Capital equipment refund claims.</span></h2>
          <p>A claim for refund for taxes paid under chapter 297A on capital equipment must be filed within 3-1/2 years from the 20th day of the month following the month of the invoice date for the purchase of the capital equipment. A claim for refund for taxes imposed on capital equipment under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.63">297A.63</a> must be filed within 3-1/2 years from the date prescribed for filing the return, or one year from the date of an order assessing tax under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.33">270C.33</a>, upon payment in full of the tax, penalties, and interest shown on the order, whichever period expires later.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.289A.40.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/480/#laws.1.20.0">1990 c 480 art 1 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/291/#laws.6.46.0">1991 c 291 art 6 s 46</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/511/#laws.6.19.0">1992 c 511 art 6 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/375/#laws.8.14.0">1993 c 375 art 8 s 14</a>; art 10 s 18; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/587/#laws.1.24.0">1994 c 587 art 1 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/264/#laws.13.11.0">1995 c 264 art 13 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/471/#laws.2.7.0">1996 c 471 art 2 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/84/#laws.3.1.0">1997 c 84 art 3 s 1</a>; art 6 s 24; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/243/#laws.16.15.0">1999 c 243 art 16 s 15</a>,16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/7/#laws.0.57.0">2001 c 7 s 57</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/127/#laws.1.4.0">2003 c 127 art 1 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.2.17.0">2005 c 151 art 2 s 17</a>; art 7 s 3-5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/154/#laws.12.1.0">2008 c 154 art 12 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/86/#laws.1.54.0">2009 c 86 art 1 s 54</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.289A.41">
        <h1 class="shn">289A.41 BANKRUPTCY; SUSPENSION OF TIME.</h1>
        <p>The running of the period during which a tax must be assessed or collection proceedings commenced is suspended during the period from the date of a filing of a petition in bankruptcy until 30 days after either notice to the commissioner of revenue that the bankruptcy proceedings have been closed or dismissed, or notice that the automatic stay has been terminated or has expired, whichever occurs first.</p>
        <p>The suspension of the statute of limitations under this section applies to the person the petition in bankruptcy is filed against and other persons who may also be wholly or partially liable for the tax.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.289A.41.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/480/#laws.1.21.0">1990 c 480 art 1 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/88/#laws.11.6.0">2009 c 88 art 11 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.289A.42">
        <h1 class="shn">289A.42 CONSENT TO EXTEND STATUTE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.42.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Extension agreement.</span></h2>
          <p>If before the expiration of time prescribed in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.38">289A.38</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.382">289A.382</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.40">289A.40</a> for the assessment of tax or the filing of a claim for refund, both the commissioner and the taxpayer have consented in writing to the assessment or filing of a claim for refund after that time, the tax may be assessed or the claim for refund filed at any time before the expiration of the agreed-upon period. The period may be extended by later agreements in writing before the expiration of the period previously agreed upon. The taxpayer and the commissioner may also agree to extend the period for collection of the tax.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.42.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Federal extensions.</span></h2>
          <p>When a taxpayer consents to an extension of time for the assessment of federal withholding or income taxes, the period in which the commissioner may recompute the tax is also extended, notwithstanding any period of limitations to the contrary, as follows:</p>
          <p>(1) for the periods provided in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.38">289A.38</a>, subdivisions 8 and 9, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.382">289A.382, subdivisions 2</a> and 3;</p>
          <p>(2) for six months following the expiration of the extended federal period of limitations when no change is made by the federal authority. If no change is made by the federal authority, and, but for this subdivision, the commissioner's time period to adjust the tax has expired, and if the commissioner has completed a field audit of the taxpayer, no additional changes resulting in additional tax due or a refund may be made. For purposes of this subdivision, "field audit" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.38#stat.289A.38.9">289A.38, subdivision 9</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.289A.42.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/480/#laws.1.22.0">1990 c 480 art 1 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/291/#laws.6.14.0">1991 c 291 art 6 s 14</a>; art 16 s 9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/300/#laws.1.4.0">1998 c 300 art 1 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.2.9.0">2005 c 151 art 2 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/212/#laws.3.25.0">2006 c 212 art 3 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/13/#laws.3.16.0">2007 c 13 art 3 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/14/#laws.2.10.0">1Sp2021 c 14 art 2 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.289A.43"><b>289A.43</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.1.117.0">2005 c 151 art 1 s 117</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">REFUNDS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.289A.50">
        <h1 class="shn">289A.50 CLAIMS FOR REFUNDS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.50.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">General right to refund.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Subject to the requirements of this section and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.40">289A.40</a>, a taxpayer who has paid a tax in excess of the taxes lawfully due and who files a written claim for refund will be refunded or credited the overpayment of the tax determined by the commissioner to be erroneously paid.</p>
          <p>(b) The claim must specify the name of the taxpayer, the date when and the period for which the tax was paid, the kind of tax paid, the amount of the tax that the taxpayer claims was erroneously paid, the grounds on which a refund is claimed, and other information relative to the payment and in the form required by the commissioner. An income tax, estate tax, or corporate franchise tax return, or amended return claiming an overpayment constitutes a claim for refund.</p>
          <p>(c) When, in the course of an examination, and within the time for requesting a refund, the commissioner determines that there has been an overpayment of tax, the commissioner shall refund or credit the overpayment to the taxpayer and no demand is necessary. If the overpayment exceeds $1, the amount of the overpayment must be refunded to the taxpayer. If the amount of the overpayment is less than $1, the commissioner is not required to refund. In these situations, the commissioner does not have to make written findings or serve notice by mail to the taxpayer.</p>
          <p>(d) If the amount allowable as a credit for withholding, estimated taxes, or dependent care exceeds the tax against which the credit is allowable, the amount of the excess is considered an overpayment. The refund allowed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.06#stat.290.06.23">290.06, subdivision 23</a>, is also considered an overpayment. The requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.33">270C.33</a> do not apply to the refunding of such an overpayment shown on the original return filed by a taxpayer.</p>
          <p>(e) If the entertainment tax withheld at the source exceeds by $1 or more the taxes, penalties, and interest reported in the return of the entertainment entity or imposed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.9201">290.9201</a>, the excess must be refunded to the entertainment entity. If the excess is less than $1, the commissioner need not refund that amount.</p>
          <p>(f) If the surety deposit required for a construction contract exceeds the liability of the out-of-state contractor, the commissioner shall refund the difference to the contractor.</p>
          <p>(g) An action of the commissioner in refunding the amount of the overpayment does not constitute a determination of the correctness of the return of the taxpayer.</p>
          <p>(h) There is appropriated from the general fund to the commissioner of revenue the amount necessary to pay refunds allowed under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.50.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Refund form.</span></h2>
          <p>On or before January 1, 2000, the commissioner of revenue shall prepare and make available to taxpayers a form for filing claims for refund of taxes paid in excess of the amount due. The commissioner may require corporate franchise taxpayers claiming a refund of corporate franchise taxes paid in excess of the amount lawfully due to include on the claim for refund or amended return information necessary for payment of the taxes paid in excess of taxes lawfully due by electronic means.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.50.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Refund of sales tax to vendors; limitation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If a vendor has collected from a purchaser and remitted to the state a tax on a transaction that is not subject to the tax imposed by chapter 297A, the tax is refundable to the vendor only if and to the extent that the tax and any interest earned on the tax is credited to amounts due to the vendor by the purchaser or returned to the purchaser by the vendor.</p>
          <p>(b) In addition to the requirements of subdivision 1, a claim for refund under this subdivision must state in writing that the tax and interest earned on the tax has been or will be refunded or credited to the purchaser by the vendor.</p>
          <p>(c) Within 60 days after the date the commissioner issues the refund, any amount not refunded or credited to the purchaser by the vendor, as required by paragraph (a), must be returned to the commissioner by the vendor.</p>
          <p>(d) After the commissioner refunds the tax and interest to the vendor, if the commissioner determines that the vendor did not refund or credit the tax and interest as provided in this subdivision, or did not return the amount required to be returned under paragraph (c), the commissioner may assess the vendor for underpayment of tax and interest equal to that portion of the amount that was not refunded or credited to the purchaser. The assessment bears interest which is computed at the rate specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.40#stat.270C.40.5">270C.40, subdivision 5</a>, on the unpaid amount from the date the commissioner issues the refund until the date the amount is paid to the commissioner. The assessment may be made at any time within 3-1/2 years after the commissioner refunds the tax and interest to the vendor. If part of the refund was induced by fraud or misrepresentation of a material fact, the assessment may be made at any time.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.50.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Refund of sales tax to purchasers.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If a vendor has collected from a purchaser a tax on a transaction that is not subject to the tax imposed by chapter 297A, the purchaser may apply directly to the commissioner for a refund under this section if:</p>
          <p>(1) the purchaser is currently registered or was registered during the period of the claim, to collect and remit the sales tax or to remit the use tax; and</p>
          <p>(2) either</p>
          <p>(i) the amount of the refund to be applied for exceeds $500, or</p>
          <p>(ii) the amount of the refund to be applied for does not exceed $500, but the purchaser also applies for a capital equipment claim at the same time, and the total of the two refunds exceeds $500.</p>
          <p>(b) The purchaser may not file more than two applications for refund under this subdivision in a calendar year.</p>
          <p>(c) Refunds shall not be issued for sales for resale where the vendor has a published no resale policy.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.50.2b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2b.<span class="headnote">Certified service provider; bad debt claim.</span></h2>
          <p>A certified service provider, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.995#stat.297A.995.2">297A.995, subdivision 2</a>, may claim on behalf of a taxpayer that is its client any bad debt allowance provided by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.81">297A.81</a>. The certified service provider must credit or refund to its client the full amount of any bad debt allowance or refund received.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.50.2c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2c.<span class="headnote">Notice from purchaser to vendor requesting refund.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If a vendor has collected from a purchaser a tax on a transaction that is not subject to the tax imposed by chapter 297A, the purchaser may seek from the vendor a return of over-collected sales or use taxes as follows:</p>
          <p>(1) the purchaser must provide written notice to the vendor;</p>
          <p>(2) the notice to the vendor must contain the information necessary to determine the validity of the request; and</p>
          <p>(3) no cause of action against the vendor accrues until the vendor has had 60 days to respond to the written notice.</p>
          <p>(b) In connection with a purchaser's request from a vendor of over-collected sales or use taxes, a vendor is presumed to have a reasonable business practice, if in the collection of such sales or use taxes, the vendor: (1) uses a certified service provider as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.995">297A.995</a>, a certified automated system, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/297A.995">297A.995</a>, or a proprietary system that is certified by the state; and (2) has remitted to the state all taxes collected less any deductions, credits, or collection allowances.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.50.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Withholding tax and entertainer withholding tax refunds.</span></h2>
          <p>When there is an overpayment of withholding tax by an employer or a person making royalty payments, or an overpayment of entertainer withholding tax by the payor, a refund allowable under this section is limited to the amount of the overpayment that was not deducted and withheld from employee wages or from the royalty payments, or from the compensation of an entertainer.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.50.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Nonresident withholding tax refunds.</span></h2>
          <p>When there is an overpayment of nonresident withholding tax by a partnership or S corporation, a refund allowable under this section to the payor is limited to the amount of the overpayment that was not deducted and withheld from the shares of the payor's partners or shareholders.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.50.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Notice of refund.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall determine the amount of refund, if any, that is due, and notify the taxpayer of the determination as soon as practicable after a claim has been filed.</p>
          <p>If the commissioner determines that the address provided by the taxpayer to claim a refund is invalid or is no longer the current address of the taxpayer, then the date of the mailing of the notification provided under this subdivision is considered the date that the refund is paid for purposes of the payment of interest under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.56">289A.56</a> and is considered the date of issuance of the original warrant or check for purposes of issuing a new warrant or check under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.347">270C.347</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.50.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Withholding of refunds from child support and maintenance debtors.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If a court of this state finds that a person obligated to pay child support or maintenance is delinquent in making payments, the amount of child support or maintenance unpaid and owing, including attorney fees and costs incurred in ascertaining or collecting child support or maintenance, must be withheld from a refund due the person under chapter 290. The public agency responsible for child support enforcement or the parent or guardian of a child for whom the support, attorney fees, and costs are owed or the party to whom maintenance, attorney fees, and costs are owed may petition the district court for an order providing for the withholding of the amount of child support, maintenance, attorney fees, and costs unpaid and owing as determined by court order. The person from whom the refund may be withheld must be notified of the petition under the Rules of Civil Procedure before the issuance of an order under this subdivision. The order may be granted on a showing to the court that required support or maintenance payments, attorney fees, and costs have not been paid when they were due.</p>
          <p>(b) On order of the court, the commissioner shall withhold the money from the refund due to the person obligated to pay the child support or maintenance. The amount withheld shall be remitted to the public agency responsible for child support enforcement, the parent or guardian petitioning on behalf of the child, or the party to whom maintenance is owed, after any delinquent tax obligations of the taxpayer owed to the revenue department have been satisfied and after deduction of the fee prescribed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270A.07#stat.270A.07.1">270A.07, subdivision 1</a>. An amount received by the responsible public agency, or the petitioning parent or guardian, or the party to whom maintenance is owed, in excess of the amount of public assistance spent for the benefit of the child to be supported, or the amount of any support, maintenance, attorney fees, and costs that had been the subject of the claim under this subdivision that has been paid by the taxpayer before the diversion of the refund, must be paid to the person entitled to the money. If the refund is based on a joint return, the part of the refund that must be paid to the petitioner is the proportion of the total refund that equals the proportion of the total federal adjusted gross income of the spouses that is the federal adjusted gross income of the spouse who is delinquent in making the child support or maintenance payments.</p>
          <p>(c) A petition filed under this subdivision remains in effect with respect to any refunds due under this section until the support or maintenance, attorney fees, and costs have been paid in full or the court orders the commissioner to discontinue withholding the money from the refund due the person obligated to pay the support or maintenance, attorney fees, and costs. If a petition is filed under this subdivision concerning child support and a claim is made under chapter 270A with respect to the individual's refund and notices of both are received before the time when payment of the refund is made on either claim, the claim relating to the liability that accrued first in time must be paid first. The amount of the refund remaining must then be applied to the other claim.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.50.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/389/#laws.6.20.0">1998 c 389 art 6 s 20</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.50.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Remedies.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If the taxpayer is notified by the commissioner that the refund claim is denied in whole or in part, the taxpayer may:</p>
          <p>(1) file an administrative appeal as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.35">270C.35</a>, or an appeal with the Tax Court, within 60 days after the notice date of the commissioner's notice of denial; or</p>
          <p>(2) file an action in the district court to recover the refund.</p>
          <p>(b) An action in the district court on a denied claim for refund must be brought within 18 months of the notice date of the denial of the claim by the commissioner. For the purposes of this section, "notice date" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.35#stat.270C.35.3">270C.35, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) No action in the district court or the Tax Court shall be brought within six months of the filing of the refund claim unless the commissioner denies the claim within that period.</p>
          <p>(d) If a taxpayer files a claim for refund and the commissioner has not issued a denial of the claim, the taxpayer may bring an action in the district court or the Tax Court at any time after the expiration of six months from the time the claim was filed.</p>
          <p>(e) The commissioner and the taxpayer may agree to extend the period for bringing an action in the district court.</p>
          <p>(f) An action for refund of tax by the taxpayer must be brought in the district court of the district in which lies the county of the taxpayer's residence or principal place of business. In the case of an estate or trust, the action must be brought at the principal place of its administration. Any action may be brought in the district court for Ramsey County.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.50.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Mistake discovered by commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>If money has been erroneously collected from a taxpayer or other person, the commissioner shall, within the period named in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/289A.40">289A.40</a> for filing a claim for refund, and, subject to the provisions of chapter 270A, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.64">270C.64</a>, and this section, grant a refund to that taxpayer or other person.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.50.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Petition in Tax Court; refund of interest.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding any other law, within one year after a decision of the Tax Court upholding an assessment of the commissioner of revenue becomes final, if the taxpayer has paid the assessment in full, plus interest calculated by the commissioner, the taxpayer may petition the Tax Court to reopen the case solely for a determination that the interest paid exceeds the interest legally due, and if so, the amount of the overpayment. A determination of overpayment of interest under this subdivision is a determination of overpayment of tax under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/271.12">271.12</a>, and is reviewable in the same manner as any other decision of the Tax Court.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.50.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Limitation on refund.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If an addition to federal taxable income under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.0131#stat.290.0131.2">290.0131, subdivision 2</a>, is judicially determined to discriminate against interstate commerce with respect to obligations of a certain character or type, the legislature intends that the discrimination be remedied by adding to federal taxable income interest on comparable obligations of Minnesota governmental units and Indian tribes. For purposes of this subdivision, "comparable obligation" means obligations of the character or type that the court found to be unconstitutionally favored by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.0131">290.0131</a>, subdivision 2, whether based on the security for payment, use of the proceeds, or any other factor identified as determinative by the court.</p>
          <p>(b) This subdivision applies beginning with the taxable years that begin during the calendar year in which the court's decision is final. Other remedies apply for previous taxable years.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.289A.50.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/480/#laws.1.23.0">1990 c 480 art 1 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/604/#laws.1.21.0">1990 c 604 art 1 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/291/#laws.6.15.0">1991 c 291 art 6 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/511/#laws.7.12.0">1992 c 511 art 7 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/322/#laws.0.6.0">1993 c 322 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/375/#laws.8.4.0">1993 c 375 art 8 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/264/#laws.1.1.0">1995 c 264 art 1 s 1</a>; art 19 s 5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/471/#laws.2.8.0">1996 c 471 art 2 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/243/#laws.16.17.0">1999 c 243 art 16 s 17</a>,18; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/7/#laws.0.58.0">2001 c 7 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/5/#laws.7.33.0">1Sp2001 c 5 art 7 s 33</a>; art 12 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/127/#laws.1.5.0">2003 c 127 art 1 s 5</a>,6; art 6 s 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/151/#laws.2.17.0">2005 c 151 art 2 s 17</a>; art 6 s 7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/389/#laws.3.6.0">2010 c 389 art 3 s 6</a>; art 4 s 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/112/#laws.1.9.0">2011 c 112 art 1 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.3.5.0">2016 c 158 art 3 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/1/#laws.16.28.0">1Sp2017 c 1 art 16 s 28</a>; art 21 s 6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/64/#laws.16.1.0">2023 c 64 art 16 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.289A.51">
        <h1 class="shn">289A.51 ELECTRIC-ASSISTED BICYCLE REBATE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.51.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Electric-assisted bicycle" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/169.011">169.011, subdivision </a>27, except that the term is limited to a new electric-assisted bicycle purchased from an eligible retailer.</p>
          <p>(c) "Eligible expenses" means the amount paid for an electric-assisted bicycle and any qualifying accessories purchased at the same time as the electric-assisted bicycle, inclusive of sales tax but exclusive of any other related charges, including charges for a warranty, service, or delivery.</p>
          <p>(d) "Eligible individual" means an individual who:</p>
          <p>(1) is at least 15 years old;</p>
          <p>(2) is a resident individual taxpayer at the time of application for a rebate certificate and in the previous calendar year; and</p>
          <p>(3) was not claimed as a dependent on another return in the taxable year described in subdivision 3, paragraph (c).</p>
          <p>(e) "Eligible retailer" means a person who has engaged in the business of retail sales of new electric-assisted bicycles for at least six months prior to receiving the approval of the commissioner under subdivision 5.</p>
          <p>(f) "Qualifying accessories" means a bicycle helmet, lights, lock, luggage rack, basket, bag or backpack, fenders, or reflective clothing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.51.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Rebate established.</span></h2>
          <p>An eligible retailer that has been assigned a rebate certificate by an eligible individual may apply to the commissioner for a rebate, as provided in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.51.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Amount of rebate.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The amount of a rebate under this section equals the lesser of:</p>
          <p>(1) the applicable percentage, multiplied by the amount of eligible expenses paid by an eligible individual; or</p>
          <p>(2) $1,500.</p>
          <p>(b) The applicable percentage equals 75 percent, but is reduced by one percentage point until the percentage equals 50 percent, for each $4,000 of the eligible individual's adjusted gross income in excess of:</p>
          <p>(1) $50,000 for a married taxpayer filing a joint return; and</p>
          <p>(2) $25,000 for all other filers.</p>
          <p>(c) For the purposes of determining the applicable percentage under paragraph (b) and subdivision 4, paragraph (a), the commissioner must use the eligible individual's adjusted gross income for the taxable year ending in the calendar year prior to the year in which the individual applied for a rebate certificate.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.289A.51.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Commissioner to issue rebate certificates.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) To qualify for a rebate under this section, an eligible individual must apply to the commissioner for a rebate certificate in the manner specified by the commissioner prior to purchasing an electric-assisted bicycle.
